  • an Ok result on success; an error with is_not_found() when from (or the parent of to) does not exist or, on POSIX, either path is empty, is_permission_denied() when access is refused, or another platform error

Example

    File.rename(Path("draft.txt"), Path("final.txt"));

remove

static IoResult<void> remove(Path path)

Deletes the file at path. A symlink is removed itself, not its target. Directories are not removed: use Directory.remove or Directory.remove_all.

Parameters

  • path — the file to delete

Returns

  • an Ok result on success; an error with is_not_found() when the path does not exist (on POSIX, also when it is empty); when it is a directory, is_is_a_directory() on Linux and is_permission_denied() on macOS and Windows; is_permission_denied() when access is refused (on Windows also for a read-only file); or another platform error

Example

    var r = File.remove(Path("old.txt"));
    if (r.is_err() && !r.error().is_not_found()) {
        return 1;
    }

Methods

is_open

bool is_open()

Returns whether the File holds a valid handle: true after a successful open, false after close() and for a File() never opened.

Returns

  • true while the File is open

check_open

IoResult<void> check_open()

Checks that the File is open, for use before an operation.

Returns

  • an Ok result when the File is open; an error with the message “file is closed” otherwise (code EBADF on POSIX, ERROR_INVALID_HANDLE on Windows; no IoError predicate holds)

read

IoResult<ulong> read(u8[] buf, ulong max)

Reads up to max bytes from the current position and appends them to buf, growing it; the bytes already in buf are kept. Advances the position by the count read.

One system call: the read may be partial, returning fewer than max bytes before the end of the file (always so for pipes and terminals), and a single call moves at most about 2 GiB on POSIX or 1 GiB on Windows. Loop until it returns 0 to read everything, or use read_all. On POSIX an interrupted read (EINTR) is retried. On failure buf keeps its previous contents.

Parameters

  • buf — the array the bytes are appended to
  • max — the most bytes to read; 0 reads nothing and returns 0

Returns

  • the number of bytes appended, 0 at end of file; an error when the File is closed, or is_is_a_directory() when it is a directory, or another platform error

Example

    var buf = Array<u8>();
    while (true) {
        var r = f.read(buf, 4096);
        if (r.is_err() || r.value() == 0) {
            break;
        }
    }

read_all

IoResult<u8[]> read_all()

Reads everything from the current position to the end of the file, in 64 KiB chunks, leaving the position at the end.

Returns

  • a new array of the bytes read (empty at end of file); an error when the File is closed, is_is_a_directory() when it is a directory, or another platform error, in which case the bytes read so far are discarded

read_string

IoResult<String> read_string()

Reads everything from the current position to the end of the file, as read_all does, and returns it as UTF-8 text.

The bytes must be strictly valid UTF-8 (RFC 3629: no overlong forms, surrogates, or code points past U+10FFFF). A byte-order mark is kept as part of the text.

Returns

  • the text read (empty at end of file); an error with is_invalid_input() when the bytes are not valid UTF-8, or any error read_all returns

write

IoResult<ulong> write(u8[] data)

Writes bytes from data at the current position (or at the end, in append mode) with a single system call, and advances the position.

The write may be partial: it can write fewer than data.count() bytes, and one call moves at most about 2 GiB on POSIX or 1 GiB on Windows. Use write_all to write everything. On POSIX an interrupted write (EINTR) is retried.

Parameters

  • data — the bytes to write

Returns

  • the number of bytes written, which may be less than data.count(); an error when the File is closed or was not opened for writing, or another platform error

write_all

IoResult<void> write_all(u8[] data)

Writes all of data, repeating the write until every byte is written. Advances the position by data.count().

If an error occurs part of data may already have been written.

Parameters

  • data — the bytes to write

Returns

  • an Ok result once every byte is written; an error when the File is closed or was not opened for writing, when a write makes no progress (“failed to write the whole buffer”, with code EIO on POSIX or ERROR_WRITE_FAULT on Windows), or another platform error

write_string

IoResult<void> write_string(String s)

Writes all of the UTF-8 bytes of s, as write_all does. No newline or terminator is added.

Parameters

  • s — the text to write

Returns

  • an Ok result once every byte is written; an error as for write_all

Example

    var f = File.create(Path("greeting.txt")).value();
    var r = f.write_string("hello, world");
    if (r.is_err()) {
        return 1;
    }

seek_start

IoResult<Offset> seek_start(Offset pos)

Moves the position to pos bytes from the start of the file.

Seeking past the end is allowed; a later write there fills the gap with zeros.

Parameters

  • pos — the new position, in bytes from the start

Returns

  • the new position, in bytes from the start of the file; an error as for seek

See also

  • seek

seek_end

IoResult<Offset> seek_end(Offset delta)

Moves the position to delta bytes from the end of the file. seek_end(0) moves to the end and returns the file’s length.

Parameters

  • delta — the offset from the end of the file, in bytes; negative moves back from the end

Returns

  • the new position, in bytes from the start of the file; an error as for seek

See also

  • seek

seek_current

IoResult<Offset> seek_current(Offset delta)

Moves the position by delta bytes from where it is. seek_current(0) returns the current position without moving it.

Parameters

  • delta — the offset from the current position, in bytes; negative moves back

Returns

  • the new position, in bytes from the start of the file; an error as for seek

See also

  • seek

seek

IoResult<Offset> seek(Offset offset, int whence)

Moves the position by offset relative to the origin whence. seek_start, seek_end and seek_current name the three origins.

A position before the start of the file is an error. Seeking a stream that has no position (a pipe or a terminal) fails on POSIX and has platform-defined results on Windows.

Parameters

  • offset — the offset in bytes from the origin; may be negative
  • whence — the origin: 0 the start of the file, 1 the current position, 2 the end of the file (SEEK_SET, SEEK_CUR, SEEK_END on POSIX; FILE_BEGIN, FILE_CURRENT, FILE_END on Windows)

Returns

  • the new position, in bytes from the start of the file; an error when the File is closed, whence is not 0, 1 or 2, the resulting position would be negative (on POSIX, with is_invalid_input()), or the stream cannot seek

set_length

IoResult<void> set_length(ulong size)

Truncates or extends the file to exactly size bytes; bytes added are zeros. The position is kept, even when it is now past the end.

The File must have been opened for writing.

Parameters

  • size — the new length, in bytes

Returns

  • an Ok result on success; an error when the File is closed, was not opened for writing, or the system refuses the size

set_len

[Deprecated("use 'set_length' instead", "set_length")] IoResult<void> set_len(ulong size)

Deprecated: use set_length instead. Truncates or extends the file to exactly size bytes.

Parameters

  • size — the new length, in bytes

Returns

  • the same as set_length(size)

sync

IoResult<void> sync()

Flushes the file’s contents and metadata to the storage device, waiting until they are written (fsync on POSIX, FlushFileBuffers on Windows). Writes are already unbuffered in the process; this forces them out of the operating system’s cache.

On Windows the File must have been opened with write access.

Returns

  • an Ok result once the data is durable; an error when the File is closed or the device reports a failure, or another platform error

metadata

IoResult<Metadata> metadata()

Queries the metadata of the open file itself (the target, when it was opened through a symlink): size, kind, read-only flag, permission bits and timestamps.

Returns

  • the file’s Metadata; an error when the File is closed, or another platform error

Example

    var size = f.metadata().value().size;

close

IoResult<void> close()

Closes the File now, to observe the error the system reports (for example a deferred write failure). Without it the destructor closes the handle and ignores errors.

Idempotent: closing a closed File succeeds. The File counts as closed afterwards even when closing reports an error, so the handle is never closed twice. A standard stream is only marked closed: the stream stays open, and File.stdout() and the like still work.

Returns

  • an Ok result on success or when already closed; the platform error when closing an owned handle fails

Example

    var f = File.create(Path("out.bin")).value();
    f.write_string("data");
    if (f.close().is_err()) {
        return 1;
    }

Directory

Source: runtime/io/directory.pep

DirEntry

struct DirEntry

One entry of a directory listing, as Directory.entries() returns it.

The kind flags come from the listing itself and describe the entry without following symlinks. On POSIX a symlink has is_symlink set and is_file and is_directory clear; on Windows a symlink or junction to a directory has both is_directory and is_symlink set. Entries that are none of these (a FIFO, socket or device on POSIX) have all three flags clear. Call metadata() for the details of what the entry points to.

Example

    import io;

    var list = io.Directory.open(io.Path("src")).value().entries().value();
    ulong i = 0;
    while (i < list.count()) {
        if (list[i].is_file) {
            print(list[i].Name);
        }
        i = i + 1;
    }

Fields

path

Path path

The entry’s full path: the listed directory’s path joined with name (relative when the directory’s path was relative).

name

String name

The entry’s own file name, without any directory part.

is_file

bool is_file

Whether the entry is a regular file (never true for a symlink).

is_directory

bool is_directory

Whether the entry is a directory. On POSIX never true for a symlink; on Windows also true for a symlink or junction to a directory.

is_symlink

bool is_symlink

Whether the entry is a symlink (on Windows, any reparse point, junctions included), which is not followed.

Constructor

DirEntry()

construct()

Creates an empty entry: empty path and name, all kind flags false. Entries are normally obtained from Directory.entries().

Methods

metadata

IoResult<Metadata> metadata()

Returns the metadata of the entry, following symlinks, exactly like Path.metadata() on the entry’s path. The file system is queried again now, so the result reflects any change since the listing was read.

Returns

  • the entry’s Metadata (of the target, for a symlink); an error when it cannot be stat’ed, with is_not_found() when the entry was removed since the listing or is a dangling symlink

See also

  • Path.metadata

Directory

struct Directory

A directory on the file system, identified by its path, together with static functions that create, remove and inspect directories and the process’s current and temporary directories.

A Directory holds no operating-system handle: open only checks that the path is a directory, and each call to entries() reads the listing afresh.

Example

    import io;

    var dir = io.Directory.temp().join("scratch").join("logs");
    if (io.Directory.create_all(dir).is_ok()) {
        var d = io.Directory.open(dir).value();
        var count = d.entries().value().count();
        io.Directory.remove_all(io.Directory.temp().join("scratch"));
    }

Fields

path

Path path

The directory’s path, as given to open.

Constructor

Directory()

construct()

Creates a Directory with an empty path, which names no directory. Use Directory.open to get one for an existing directory.

Static methods

open

static IoResult<Directory> open(Path path)

Checks that path exists and is a directory (following symlinks) and returns a Directory for it. Nothing is kept open, so the directory can still be renamed or removed afterwards.

Parameters

  • path — the directory’s path; a relative path is resolved against the current directory whenever the Directory is used

Returns

  • the Directory; an error when path cannot be stat’ed (is_not_found() for a missing or empty path, is_permission_denied()) or is not a directory (is_not_a_directory(), message "not a directory")

Example

    import io;

    var d = io.Directory.open(io.Path("src"));
    if (d.is_err() && d.error().is_not_a_directory()) {
        print("src is a file");
    }

create

static IoResult<void> create(Path path)

Creates one directory (mkdir on POSIX, with mode 0777 less the umask; CreateDirectoryW on Windows). Its parent must already exist.

Parameters

  • path — the directory to create

Returns

  • an Ok result on success; an error when the directory cannot be created: is_already_exists() when anything (a file too) is already at path, is_not_found() when the parent is missing or the path is empty, is_permission_denied() when the parent is not writable

See also

  • create_all

create_all

static IoResult<void> create_all(Path path)

Creates a directory and any missing parents, like mkdir -p. Succeeds without doing anything when path is empty or already a directory (or a symlink to one). Parents are found lexically with Path.parent(); a relative path is created under the current directory. It is safe against another process creating the same directories at the same time.

Parameters

  • path — the directory to create, with its parents

Returns

  • an Ok result on success; an error when a directory cannot be created: is_already_exists() when a file (not a directory) is at path or at one of its parents, is_permission_denied(), and so on. Parents created before the failure are left in place

Example

    import io;

    var r = io.Directory.create_all(io.Path("build").join("out").join("logs"));

remove

static IoResult<void> remove(Path path)

Removes an empty directory (rmdir on POSIX, RemoveDirectoryW on Windows).

Parameters

  • path — the directory to remove; it must be empty

Returns

  • an Ok result on success; an error when it cannot be removed: is_directory_not_empty() when it still has entries, is_not_found() when it is missing or the path is empty, is_not_a_directory() when path is a file, is_permission_denied()

See also

  • remove_all

remove_all

static IoResult<void> remove_all(Path path)

Removes path and, if it is a directory, everything inside it, recursively. Symlinks are removed, never followed, so nothing outside the tree is touched. A file or symlink at path itself is simply removed.

Stops at the first failure, leaving whatever was not yet removed; on Windows a read-only file inside the tree is such a failure (is_permission_denied()).

Parameters

  • path — the file, symlink or directory tree to remove

Returns

  • an Ok result on success; an error when something cannot be removed: is_not_found() when path does not exist (unlike rm -rf, a missing path is an error) or is empty, is_permission_denied(), and so on

See also

  • remove

Example

    import io;

    var tree = io.Directory.temp().join("my-scratch");
    var r = io.Directory.remove_all(tree);
    if (r.is_err() && !r.error().is_not_found()) {
        print(r.error().message);
    }

current

static IoResult<Path> current()

Returns the process’s current directory, against which relative paths are resolved (getcwd on POSIX, GetCurrentDirectoryW on Windows).

Returns

  • the current directory as an absolute path; an error when it cannot be determined, such as is_not_found() on POSIX when the directory has been removed

See also

  • set_current

set_current

static IoResult<void> set_current(Path path)

Changes the process’s current directory (chdir on POSIX, SetCurrentDirectoryW on Windows). This affects the whole process, every thread included: relative paths used anywhere afterwards resolve against the new directory.

Parameters

  • path — the new current directory; a relative path is resolved against the old one

Returns

  • an Ok result on success; an error when the directory cannot be entered: is_not_found() when it is missing or the path is empty, is_not_a_directory() when it is a file, is_permission_denied()

See also

  • current

temp

static Path temp()

Returns the directory for temporary files. It is not created or checked, so it may not exist.

On POSIX it is $TMPDIR with any trailing / removed, or /tmp when TMPDIR is unset or empty. On Windows it is what GetTempPathW reports (from TMP, TEMP, USERPROFILE, or the Windows directory) with its trailing separator removed unless it is a root, or . if that call fails.

Returns

  • the temporary directory’s path, never an error

Example

    import io;

    var scratch = io.Directory.temp().join("my-tool");

Methods

entries

IoResult<DirEntry[]> entries()

Reads the directory’s listing now, all at once: every entry except . and .., in the order the operating system returns them (not sorted). Uses opendir/readdir on POSIX and FindFirstFileW/FindNextFileW on Windows.

Returns

  • the entries, possibly none; an error when the directory cannot be read: is_not_found() when it no longer exists (or, on POSIX, path is empty), is_permission_denied(), or on POSIX is_not_a_directory() when path now names a file

Example

    import io;

    var list = io.Directory.open(io.Path(".")).value().entries();
    if (list.is_ok()) {
        var n = list.value().count();
    }

The async module

Threads and concurrency, modeled on Rust’s std::thread and C++ std::thread.

Like io, async is a module: bring it in with import async (and write async.Thread, async.logical_cpus()) or import names directly with import [Thread, ThreadLocal] from async.

  • Thread: starting, naming and joining threads, ThreadId and ThreadBody
  • ThreadLocal: a variable with a separate value on every thread
  • Processor counts: logical_cpus() and physical_cpus()
  • Channel: Channel<T>, a queue that carries values between threads, and its ChannelIterator<T>
  • Mutex: Mutex<T>, the MutexGuard<T> a with block holds, and Condition<T>
  • Once: Once, which runs a function once, and Lazy<T>, a value built on first use

Only thread-safe values can be shared between threads: numbers, shared function values (such as a thread’s body), and [Shared] structs such as Atomic<T>, Thread, ThreadLocal<T>, Channel<T> and Mutex<T>. Any other value crosses threads as a copy: Channel.send copies what it sends with copy. Mutex<T> and Lazy<T> hold thread-safe values only. See the language guide’s Threads chapter.

Example

    import async;

    const hits = Atomic<long>(0);

    work() {
        hits.fetch_add(1);
    }

    main(): int {
        var workers = Array<async.Thread>();
        var n = async.logical_cpus();
        var i: ulong = 0;
        while (i < n) {
            workers.append(async.Thread.spawn(work));
            i = i + 1;
        }
        for (var worker in workers) {
            worker.join();
        }
        assert(hits.load() == n as long, "every worker ran");
        return 0;
    }

Source: runtime/async/module.pep

Thread

Threads: starting, naming, identifying and joining operating-system threads.

Source: runtime/async/thread.pep

ThreadId

type ThreadId = ulong

Identifies a thread for as long as the program runs: every thread, whether started with Thread.spawn or not, gets a distinct id the first time it is asked for one, and ids are never reused.

Ids are assigned by the runtime in the order threads ask for them, starting at 1; they are not the operating system’s thread ids. A ThreadId is a number, so it can be compared, hashed and used as a HashMap key.

See also

  • Thread.id

ThreadBody

type ThreadBody = shared () => void

The type of a thread’s body: a function that takes no arguments and returns nothing. Thread.spawn runs one on a new thread.

It is a shared function type, because the body runs on another thread: a named function, or a closure that captures only thread-safe values (numbers, Atomic<T>, [Shared] structs, other shared function values). A closure capturing a String, say, is a compile error.

See also

  • Thread.spawn

Thread

[Shared] struct Thread

An operating-system thread.

Start one with Thread.spawn, which runs a function on a new thread and returns its Thread, and wait for it to finish with join(). Every thread, including the main thread, can get its own Thread with Thread.current().

A Thread is [Shared]: the thread that started it and the thread itself can both hold it. Dropping every reference without joining detaches the thread: it keeps running, and its resources are freed when it finishes.

The body runs until it returns; there is no way to stop a thread from the outside. A panic or failed assert on any thread ends the whole program.

Example

    import async;

    const total = Atomic<long>(0);

    add_ten() {
        total.fetch_add(10);
    }

    main(): int {
        var a = async.Thread.spawn(add_ten, "adder a");
        var b = async.Thread.spawn(add_ten, "adder b");
        a.join();
        b.join();
        assert(total.load() == 20, "both threads ran");
        return 0;
    }

Fields

Id

const Id: ThreadId

The thread’s id.

NameData

const NameData: __ptr<char>

The name’s UTF-8 bytes, NUL-terminated, or null for an unnamed thread.

NameSize

const NameSize: ulong

The name’s size in bytes.

NameLength

const NameLength: ulong

The name’s length in code points.

Body

const Body: ThreadBody

The function the thread runs (a no-op for a thread that was not started by spawn, such as the main thread).

Handle

const Handle: Atomic<__ptr<void>>

The operating-system handle while the thread can still be joined or detached (a pthread_t or a Windows HANDLE), else null.

Finished

const Finished: Atomic<int>

1 once the body has returned, else 0.

Constructor

Thread()

construct(String name, ThreadBody body)

Creates a Thread value for a thread that is already running. Programs get threads from spawn and current() instead; a Thread made directly is not attached to any thread and cannot be joined.

Parameters

  • name — the thread’s name; empty for an unnamed thread
  • body — the function the thread runs

Static methods

spawn

static Thread spawn(ThreadBody body, String name = "")

Starts a new thread that runs body.

The new thread starts right away and runs until body returns. Its Thread.current() is the Thread returned here.

Parameters

  • body — the function to run on the new thread
  • name — a name for the thread, returned by name(); empty by default

Returns

  • the new thread, to join() or query

Panics

  • if the operating system cannot create another thread

Example

    var worker = Thread.spawn(do_work, "worker");
    worker.join();

current

static Thread current()

Returns the Thread of the thread that calls it.

For a thread started with spawn this is the Thread that spawn returned. Any other thread (the main thread, or one started by C code) gets a Thread the first time it asks and the same one afterwards; the main thread’s is named main, the others are unnamed. Such a Thread cannot be joined.

Returns

  • the calling thread

Example

    var me = Thread.current();
    print(me.name(), "\n");   # "main" on the main thread

sleep

static void sleep(ulong milliseconds)

Pauses the calling thread for at least milliseconds milliseconds.

The operating system may sleep longer, but never shorter.

Parameters

  • milliseconds — how long to sleep; 0 returns right away

Methods

id

ThreadId id()

Returns the thread’s id, which no other thread in the program has.

Returns

  • the thread’s id

name

String name()

Returns the name the thread was given.

Returns

  • the name passed to spawn, main for the main thread, or an empty String for an unnamed thread

is_finished

bool is_finished()

Reports whether the thread’s body has returned.

A thread that is not finished may finish at any moment, so a false result can be out of date by the time it is used. Always false for a Thread that was not started by spawn.

Returns

  • true once the body has returned

is_joinable

bool is_joinable()

Reports whether join() can be called: the thread was started by spawn and has not been joined yet.

Returns

  • true if the thread can be joined

join

void join()

Waits for the thread’s body to return.

Returns right away if it already has. A thread can be joined once; a Thread from current() for a thread not started by spawn cannot be joined at all. Everything the thread did before it finished is visible to the caller after join() returns.

Panics

  • if the thread cannot be joined (see is_joinable()), or if a thread tries to join itself

ThreadLocal

Per-thread values: ThreadLocal<T> gives every thread its own copy of a variable.

Source: runtime/async/thread_local.pep

ThreadLocal

[Shared] struct ThreadLocal<T>

A variable with a separate value on every thread, like C++ thread_local or Rust’s thread_local!.

Each thread that calls set stores its own value, and get returns the calling thread’s value (the default of T, zero or null, until that thread sets one). When a thread exits, its value is released.

A ThreadLocal is [Shared], so one object can be used from every thread; it is usually a module-scope variable. T does not have to be thread-safe, because each value stays on the thread that stored it.

Destroying a ThreadLocal releases the calling thread’s value only. Values that other threads still hold are released on Windows and leak elsewhere, so destroy one only after the threads that used it have exited.

Type parameters

  • T — the type of the per-thread value

Example

    import async;

    const calls = async.ThreadLocal<int>();

    count_call() {
        calls.set(calls.get() + 1);
    }

    main(): int {
        count_call();
        count_call();
        assert(calls.get() == 2, "two calls on this thread");
        return 0;
    }

Fields

Key

const Key: ulong

The operating-system thread-local key.

Constructor

ThreadLocal()

construct()

Creates a thread-local variable. No thread has a value yet.

Panics

  • if the operating system has no thread-local keys left

Methods

get

T get()

Returns the calling thread’s value.

For a struct T the caller gets its own reference.

Returns

  • the value this thread last stored, or the default of T (zero or null) if it has not stored one

set

void set(T value)

Stores value as the calling thread’s value, replacing (and, for a struct T, releasing) the one it held. Other threads’ values are not affected.

Parameters

  • value — the new value for this thread

is_set

bool is_set()

Reports whether the calling thread has stored a value since it started or last called clear().

Returns

  • true if this thread has a value

clear

void clear()

Removes the calling thread’s value, releasing it for a struct T. get() returns the default of T afterwards.

Processor counts

Processor counts.

Source: runtime/async/cpus.pep

logical_cpus

logical_cpus(): ulong

Returns the number of logical processors the operating system can run threads on: every hardware thread, so a core with hyperthreading (SMT) counts two or more times.

This is the upper bound on how many threads can run at the same moment, and a sensible default size for a pool of CPU-bound worker threads. Inside a virtual machine or container it counts the processors the guest sees.

Returns

  • the number of logical processors, at least 1

See also

  • physical_cpus

Example

    import async;

    main(): int {
        print(async.logical_cpus(), " logical processors\n");
        return 0;
    }

physical_cpus

physical_cpus(): ulong

Returns the number of physical processor cores: hardware threads that share a core through hyperthreading (SMT) count once.

On Linux the count comes from the kernel’s CPU topology (/sys/devices/system/cpu), on macOS from hw.physicalcpu, and on Windows from GetLogicalProcessorInformationEx. Inside a virtual machine the guest cannot see the host’s hardware, so this counts the cores the hypervisor presents. Where the topology is not available, it returns logical_cpus().

Returns

  • the number of physical cores, at least 1 and at most logical_cpus()

See also

  • logical_cpus

async/channel.pep

Channels: Channel<T> carries values from threads that send to threads that receive, and ChannelIterator<T> receives in a for loop.

Source: runtime/async/channel.pep

Channel

[Shared] struct Channel<T>

A queue that carries values from threads that send to threads that receive, like Go channels or Rust’s mpsc, with any number of each.

Values arrive in the order they were sent (from any one sender). A bounded channel holds at most capacity values, and send waits while it is full; Channel<T>(0), the default, is unbounded and send never waits.

close() ends the stream: later sends fail, and receivers take what is left and then get an error. Only close() closes a channel; dropping it or the threads that use it does not. for (var v in channel) receives until the channel is closed and empty.

send copies each value with copy, so the receiver gets a value that nothing on the sending thread references: a String or an Array can be sent, and changing the original afterwards does not affect what was sent. A thread-safe value (a number, a [Shared] struct, a shared function value such as a ThreadBody) is not copied but passed by reference. A type that copy cannot copy (a struct with a destructor or a raw pointer field that does not implement Copyable<T>, a plain function value, a trait value) is a compile error where the Channel is named.

A Channel is [Shared]: keep it in a module-scope variable or pass it to threads.

Type parameters

  • T — the type of the values carried

Example

    import async;

    const jobs = async.Channel<long>(64);
    const total = Atomic<long>(0);

    worker() {
        for (var job in jobs) {
            total.fetch_add(job);
        }
    }

    main(): int {
        var thread = async.Thread.spawn(worker);
        jobs.send(20);
        jobs.send(22);
        jobs.close();
        thread.join();
        assert(total.load() == 42, "the worker received both jobs");
        return 0;
    }

Fields

Capacity

const Capacity: ulong

The most values that may wait before send blocks, or 0 for no limit.

Lock

const Lock: __ptr<void>

The platform mutex that guards State and Buffer.

NotEmpty

const NotEmpty: __ptr<void>

The platform condition variable receivers wait on.

NotFull

const NotFull: __ptr<void>

The platform condition variable senders wait on while a bounded channel is full.

State

const State: __ptr<ulong>

The head index, the number of queued values, the number of slots in the buffer, and 1 once closed. Read and written only under Lock.

Buffer

const Buffer: __ptr<__ptr<void>>

Holds the ring buffer of values (T slots). Read and written only under Lock.

Constructor

Channel()

construct(ulong capacity = 0)

Creates an open, empty channel.

Parameters

  • capacity — how many values may wait before send blocks; 0 (the default) for no limit

Panics

  • if the operating system cannot create a mutex or condition variable

Example

    var bounded = async.Channel<long>(16);
    var unbounded = async.Channel<long>();

Methods

send

bool send(T value)

Queues a copy of value, first waiting while a bounded channel is full.

The copy is made with copy before waiting, so the value can be changed or dropped as soon as send returns; a thread-safe value is queued itself.

Parameters

  • value — the value to send

Returns

  • true if the value was queued, false if the channel is closed (the copy is dropped)

Example

    var line = "hello";
    lines.send(line);
    line.append(" again");   # the receiver still gets "hello"

try_send

bool try_send(T value)

Queues a copy of value only if there is room right now, without waiting. The copy is made even when it is not queued.

Parameters

  • value — the value to send

Returns

  • true if the value was queued, false if the channel is full or closed

receive

Result<T> receive()

Takes the next value, waiting until one arrives.

Returns

  • the value, or an error once the channel is closed and empty

Example

    var job = jobs.receive();
    if (job.is_ok()) {
        run(job.value());
    }

try_receive

Result<T> try_receive()

Takes the next value if one is waiting, without waiting.

Returns

  • the value, or an error if none is waiting (see is_closed() to tell an empty channel from a closed one)

receive_for

Result<T> receive_for(ulong milliseconds)

Like receive(), but gives up after about milliseconds milliseconds.

Parameters

  • milliseconds — the longest time to wait

Returns

  • the value, or an error if the time ran out or once the channel is closed and empty

close

void close()

Closes the channel and wakes every waiting sender and receiver.

Later sends fail. Values already queued stay, and receivers still get them; after the last one, receive() returns an error and for loops end. Closing a closed channel does nothing.

is_closed

bool is_closed()

Reports whether close() has been called.

Returns

  • true once the channel is closed, even while values are still queued

count

ulong count()

Returns the number of values waiting to be received. Other threads may change it as soon as it returns.

Returns

  • the number of queued values

iterate

ChannelIterator<T> iterate()

Returns an iterator that receives until the channel is closed and empty. A for loop over the channel calls it.

Several threads may loop over one channel at once; each value goes to exactly one of them.

Returns

  • the iterator a for loop drives

Example

    for (var line in lines) {
        print(line, "\n");
    }   # ends once `lines` is closed and empty

ChannelIterator

struct ChannelIterator<T>

Receives from a Channel<T> in a for loop: next() waits for the next value and returns false once the channel is closed and empty.

Channel.iterate() returns one; a for loop over a channel uses it without naming it. It belongs to the thread that runs the loop.

Type parameters

  • T — the type of the values carried

Example

    var values = jobs.iterate();
    while (values.next()) {
        total = total + values.current();
    }

Fields

Source

const Source: Channel<T>

The channel it receives from.

Current

T Current

The value the last successful next() received.

Constructor

ChannelIterator()

construct(Channel<T> source)

Creates an iterator that receives from source. source.iterate() does the same.

Parameters

  • source — the channel to receive from

Methods

next

bool next()

Waits for the next value.

Returns

  • true if a value arrived (read it with current()), false once the channel is closed and empty

current

T current()

Returns the value the last successful next() received.

Returns

  • the current value

async/mutex.pep

Mutual exclusion: Mutex<T> owns a value that one thread at a time can use, MutexGuard<T> is the lock a with block holds, and Condition<T> lets a thread holding a Mutex sleep until another thread changes its value.

Source: runtime/async/mutex.pep

Mutex

[Shared] struct Mutex<T>

A value that one thread at a time can use, like Rust’s Mutex<T> or a C++ std::mutex paired with the data it protects.

The Mutex owns its value, and the way to reach it is a with block: with (var value = mutex.lock()) { ... } waits until no other thread holds the lock, binds the value, and unlocks when the block ends by any path (falling off the end, return, break, continue). Everything a thread did while holding the lock is visible to the next thread that takes it.

T must be thread-safe (a number, a [Shared] struct, a shared function type); Mutex<String> is a compile error. Use a Mutex to keep several updates together: a [Shared] struct whose Atomic fields must change as a unit, or state behind a raw pointer. For a single number, Atomic<T> is simpler and faster.

A Mutex is [Shared]: keep it in a module-scope variable or pass it to threads. It is not re-entrant: locking it again on the thread that holds it panics instead of deadlocking. A panic while it is held ends the program, so a Mutex is never left “poisoned”.

Type parameters

  • T — the protected value’s type, which must be thread-safe

Example

    import async;

    [Shared]
    struct Account {
        const Checking: Atomic<long>;
        const Savings: Atomic<long>;
        construct(long checking = 0, long savings = 0) {
            Checking = Atomic<long>(checking);
            Savings = Atomic<long>(savings);
        }
    }

    const account = async.Mutex<Account>(Account(100, 0));

    save(long amount) {
        with (var a = account.lock()) {
            # No other thread sees the money in neither account.
            a.Checking.fetch_sub(amount);
            a.Savings.fetch_add(amount);
        }
    }

Fields

Lock

const Lock: __ptr<void>

The platform lock: a heap block holding a pthread_mutex_t, or a Windows SRWLOCK. It never moves, which a pthread mutex requires.

Value

const Value: __ptr<T>

A heap slot holding the value: sizeof<T>() bytes, one reference for a struct T. Read and written only while the lock is held.

Owner

const Owner: Atomic<ulong>

The operating-system id of the thread holding the lock, or 0 when the Mutex is unlocked.

Constructor

Mutex()

construct(T value)

Creates an unlocked Mutex that owns value.

For a struct T the Mutex acquires a reference to value, and releases it when the Mutex is destroyed.

Parameters

  • value — the value to protect

Panics

  • if the operating system cannot create a mutex

Methods

lock

MutexGuard<T> lock()

Waits until the calling thread holds the lock, and returns the guard that a with block enters to reach the value.

Use it as the scope of a with header: with (var v = mutex.lock()). The lock is released when the guard is destroyed, which for a guard created in the header is the end of the block. A guard kept anywhere else holds the lock until its last reference is released.

Returns

  • the guard; its enter() returns the value, and destroying it unlocks

Panics

  • if the calling thread already holds the lock

Example

    with (var account = accounts.lock()) {
        account.Balance.fetch_add(10);
    }

try_lock

MutexGuard<T> try_lock()

Takes the lock only if no thread holds it right now, without waiting.

In with (var v = mutex.try_lock()) { ... } else { ... }, the body runs when the lock was taken and the else block runs when it was not. The calling thread already holding the lock counts as busy.

Returns

  • the guard; its enter() returns the value, or null when the lock was busy

Example

    with (var account = accounts.try_lock()) {
        account.Balance.fetch_add(10);
    } else {
        print("busy; try later\n");
    }

is_held

bool is_held()

Reports whether the calling thread holds the lock.

Returns

  • true between a lock() (or a successful try_lock()) on this thread and the guard’s destruction

condition

Condition<T> condition()

Returns a new condition variable that waits on this Mutex.

Create it once and share it, as the Mutex is shared: every thread that waits or notifies must use the same Condition.

Returns

  • a new Condition bound to this Mutex

Example

    const queue = async.Mutex<Jobs>(Jobs());
    const not_empty = queue.condition();

MutexGuard

struct MutexGuard<T>

The lock on a Mutex<T> that a with block holds: enter() returns the Mutex’s value, and destroying the guard unlocks.

Get one from Mutex.lock() or Mutex.try_lock() in a with header. The block releases the guard on every path out, so the lock cannot be left held by mistake. A guard belongs to the thread that locked it; do not pass it to another thread.

Type parameters

  • T — the protected value’s type

Example

    with (var totals = stats.lock()) {
        totals.Requests.fetch_add(1);
    }   # unlocked here

Fields

Source

const Source: Mutex<T>

The Mutex this guard locks.

Locked

bool Locked

Whether the guard holds the lock: false once exit() has run, and for a try_lock() that found the lock busy.

Constructor

MutexGuard()

construct(Mutex<T> source, bool wait = true)

Locks source and creates the guard that holds it; the same as source.lock() (or source.try_lock() when wait is false).

Parameters

  • source — the Mutex to lock
  • wait — true to wait for the lock; false to take it only if it is free right now

Panics

  • if wait is true and the calling thread already holds the lock

Methods

enter

T enter()

Returns the Mutex’s value, for the with header to bind.

For a struct T the caller gets its own reference.

Returns

  • the protected value, or the default of T (null for a struct) when the guard does not hold the lock

exit

void exit()

Unlocks the Mutex if the guard holds it. The destructor calls it; calling it again does nothing.

Condition

[Shared] struct Condition<T>

Lets threads that hold a Mutex sleep until another thread changes the Mutex’s value, like C++ std::condition_variable. Get one from Mutex.condition().

wait() must run while the calling thread holds the Mutex, normally inside with (var v = mutex.lock()). It unlocks, sleeps until notified, and locks again before returning. A waiter can also wake without a notification (a spurious wakeup), so always wait in a loop that re-checks the value.

A thread that changes the value calls notify_one() or notify_all(), usually while it still holds the lock.

A Condition is [Shared], like its Mutex.

Type parameters

  • T — the Mutex’s value type

Example

    import async;

    [Shared]
    struct Jobs {
        const Waiting: Atomic<long>;
        construct() { Waiting = Atomic<long>(0); }
    }

    const jobs = async.Mutex<Jobs>(Jobs());
    const has_work = jobs.condition();

    take_job() {
        with (var j = jobs.lock()) {
            while (j.Waiting.load() == 0) {
                has_work.wait();
            }
            j.Waiting.fetch_sub(1);
        }
    }

    add_job() {
        with (var j = jobs.lock()) {
            j.Waiting.fetch_add(1);
            has_work.notify_one();
        }
    }

Fields

Mutex

const Mutex: Mutex<T>

The Mutex this condition waits on.

Handle

const Handle: __ptr<void>

The platform condition variable: a heap block holding a pthread_cond_t, or a Windows CONDITION_VARIABLE.

Constructor

Condition()

construct(Mutex<T> mutex)

Creates a condition variable that waits on mutex. mutex.condition() does the same.

Parameters

  • mutex — the Mutex that waiters hold

Panics

  • if the operating system cannot create a condition variable

Methods

wait

void wait()

Unlocks the Mutex, sleeps until another thread calls notify_one() or notify_all(), and locks the Mutex again before returning.

The thread may also wake without being notified, so call it in a loop that re-checks what it waits for.

Panics

  • if the calling thread does not hold the Mutex

Example

    with (var q = queue.lock()) {
        while (q.Waiting.load() == 0) {
            not_empty.wait();
        }
    }

wait_for

bool wait_for(ulong milliseconds)

Like wait(), but gives up after about milliseconds milliseconds. The Mutex is locked again before it returns either way.

Parameters

  • milliseconds — the longest time to sleep

Returns

  • true if the thread was woken (by a notification or spuriously), false if the time ran out

Panics

  • if the calling thread does not hold the Mutex

notify_one

void notify_one()

Wakes one thread waiting on this condition, if any. The caller does not need to hold the Mutex, but usually does, having just changed the value.

notify_all

void notify_all()

Wakes every thread waiting on this condition. Each locks the Mutex in turn before its wait() returns.

async/once.pep

One-time initialization: Once runs a function exactly once however many threads ask, and Lazy<T> builds a value on first use.

Source: runtime/async/once.pep

Once

[Shared] struct Once

Runs a function exactly once, however many threads ask, like C++ std::call_once or Rust’s std::sync::Once.

The first call runs its body. Callers that arrive while it runs wait for it to finish, and later callers return right away. Every caller sees everything the body did once call returns.

A Once is [Shared]: keep it in a module-scope variable or pass it to threads.

Example

    import async;

    const setup = async.Once();
    const setups = Atomic<long>(0);

    initialize() {
        setups.fetch_add(1);
    }

    work() {
        setup.call(initialize);   # only the first caller runs it
    }

Fields

State

const State: Atomic<int>

0 before any call, 1 while a body runs, 2 once it has finished.

Runner

const Runner: Atomic<ulong>

The operating-system id of the thread running the body, or 0.

Lock

const Lock: __ptr<void>

The platform mutex that guards the move from 0 to 1 and the wait.

Finished

const Finished: __ptr<void>

The platform condition variable that waiting callers sleep on.

Constructor

Once()

construct()

Creates a Once whose body has not run.

Panics

  • if the operating system cannot create a mutex or condition variable

Methods

call

void call(ThreadBody body)

Runs body if no call has run a body yet; otherwise waits until that run has finished. Everything the body did is visible to the caller when this returns.

Only one body ever runs, even if later calls pass different ones.

Parameters

  • body — the function to run once

Panics

  • if body itself calls call on the same Once (it would wait for itself forever)

Example

    config_once.call(load_config);

is_done

bool is_done()

Reports whether a body has finished running.

Returns

  • true once a call has completed its body on some thread

Lazy

[Shared] struct Lazy<T>

A value built on first use by whichever thread asks first, like Rust’s LazyLock. The usual way to give a module-scope variable a value that is expensive to build, or that cannot be built before main runs.

The first get() runs the initializer; threads that call get() while it runs wait for it, and every later call returns the same value.

T must be thread-safe (a number, a [Shared] struct, a shared function type), because every thread gets the same value; Lazy<String> is a compile error.

Type parameters

  • T — the value’s type, which must be thread-safe

Example

    import async;

    [Shared]
    struct Settings {
        const Workers: long;
        construct(this.Workers = 1) {}
    }

    load_settings(): Settings {
        return Settings(async.logical_cpus() as long);
    }

    const settings = async.Lazy<Settings>(load_settings);

    main(): int {
        var workers = settings.get().Workers;   # built here, on first use
        return 0;
    }

Fields

Initializer

const Initializer: shared () => T

The function that builds the value.

Value

const Value: __ptr<T>

A heap slot holding the value once it is built: sizeof<T>() bytes, one reference for a struct T.

Built

const Built: Once

Runs the initializer once.

Constructor

Lazy()

construct(shared () => T initializer)

Creates a Lazy whose value initializer builds on the first get(). Nothing runs yet.

Parameters

  • initializer — builds the value; runs at most once, on the thread that first calls get()

Panics

  • if the operating system cannot create a mutex or condition variable

Methods

get

T get()

Returns the value, building it with the initializer on the first call.

For a struct T the caller gets its own reference.

Returns

  • the value the initializer built

Panics

  • if the initializer itself calls get() on the same Lazy

is_built

bool is_built()

Reports whether the value has been built.

Returns

  • true once a get() has finished running the initializer

copy.pep

Deep copies: copy(value) makes a copy that shares no object that is not thread-safe with the original, so it can be handed to another thread; Copyable<T> lets a type say how it is copied.

Source: runtime/copy.pep

copy

copy<T>(T value): T

Returns a copy of value that shares no non-thread-safe object with it, so the copy can be handed to another thread.

Every struct reachable from value whose type is not thread-safe is copied once. Objects referenced twice stay shared inside the copy, and cycles are preserved. Thread-safe objects ([Shared] structs, shared function values) are not copied: the copy references the same ones. A weak reference to a copied object points at its copy; a weak reference to an object outside the copied value becomes null.

A struct is copied field by field, without running its constructor, unless it implements Copyable<T>. A struct with a destructor or a raw pointer field must implement Copyable<T> to be copied, since copying its fields would share what it owns; String, Array, HashMap and HashSet do. Copying such a struct that does not, a plain function value or a trait value is a compile error.

Runs in O(size of the value) and allocates each copied object.

Type parameters

  • T — the type of the value

Parameters

  • value — the value to copy

Returns

  • a copy that shares no non-thread-safe object with value; value itself when T is thread-safe

Example

    var names = Array<String>();
    names.append("ada");
    var copied = copy(names);
    copied[0].append(" lovelace");   # names[0] is still "ada"

Copyable

trait Copyable<T>

A type that says how copy copies it, for a struct that copy cannot copy field by field: one with a destructor or a raw pointer field, such as a container that owns a buffer.

Declare it with extend Buffer with Copyable<Buffer> {} and give the struct a copy(Copies copies) method. The method builds the copy and copies each value it holds with copies.copy(value), which reuses a copy already made, so objects referenced twice stay shared.

Type parameters

  • T — the implementing type

Example

    struct Buffer {
        __ptr<u8> Data;
        ulong Size;

        construct(ulong size = 0) {
            Data = _pep_malloc(size);
            Size = size;
        }

        destruct {
            _pep_free(Data);
        }

        Buffer copy(Copies copies) {
            var made = Buffer(Size);
            var i: ulong = 0;
            while (i < Size) {
                made.Data[i] = Data[i];
                i = i + 1;
            }
            return made;
        }
    }

    extend Buffer with Copyable<Buffer> {}

Methods

copy

T copy(Copies copies)

Returns a copy of this value, for copy.

Copy each struct value this one holds with copies.copy(value). A value that can hold a reference back to itself calls copies.register(this, made) before copying what it holds, so the cycle leads back to the copy.

Parameters

  • copies — the objects copied so far

Returns

  • the copy

Copies

struct Copies

The objects one copy call has copied so far, so each is copied once: objects referenced twice stay shared inside the copy, and cycles end.

copy(value) makes one and passes it to every Copyable<T> implementation it calls; programs do not create their own.

Constructor

Copies()

construct()

Creates an empty set of copies.

Methods

copy

copy<T>(value: T): T

Returns the copy of value: value itself when T is thread-safe, the copy already made when the same object was copied before, and a new copy otherwise.

Type parameters

  • T — the type of the value

Parameters

  • value — the value to copy

Returns

  • the copy

Example

    Pair copy(Copies copies) {
        return Pair(copies.copy(first), copies.copy(second));
    }

register

register<T>(original: T, made: T)

Records made as the copy of original, so that copying original again returns made. A Copyable<T> implementation calls it before copying the values it holds when one of them may lead back to it.

Type parameters

  • T — the type of the value, a struct

Parameters

  • original — the object being copied
  • made — its copy

Building this documentation

This book lives in docs/ and is rendered with mdBook. The standard library reference (docs/src/reference/) is generated from the runtime’s doc comments by pepper-docgen, a tool in this repository.

cargo install mdbook --locked                 # once

cargo run -p pepper-docgen -- serve           # live preview while writing
cargo run -p pepper-docgen -- reference       # regenerate the reference after changing runtime docs
cargo run -p pepper-docgen -- build           # render into target/docs-site/dev/
cargo run -p pepper-docgen -- build --version 0.2.0 --site <dir>

Keeping the book current

Documentation changes land in the same PR as the code they describe. The checklist of what to update for each kind of change is in AGENTS.md, under “Keep the documentation current”.

Examples are tested

Every runnable example lives in docs/examples/: a single name.pep, or a folder name/ whose entry point is main.pep. Chapters pull them in with mdBook’s include syntax, so the text on the page is the file that was tested:

```pepper
{{#include ../../examples/structs.pep}}
```

crates/pepper-driver/tests/integration/docs_examples_tests.rs compiles and runs every example; each must return 0 from main and uses assert to check what it shows. Short fragments that are not whole programs can stay inline in the chapter.

Versions

Documentation is versioned with the compiler’s semantic version (version in the root Cargo.toml). pepper-docgen build --version X.Y.Z renders the book into <site>/X.Y.Z/ and leaves every other version in the site alone; versions.json lists them for the version picker, and the site’s index.html opens the newest release. Builds from main publish as dev.

The Docs workflow (.github/workflows/docs.yml) publishes to the gh-pages branch: pushing to main updates dev, and pushing a tag vX.Y.Z adds the X.Y.Z docs. Published versions are never deleted.

The reference stays current

cargo run -p pepper-docgen -- check fails when docs/src/reference/ does not match the runtime, and CI runs it. The runtime itself must meet the doc comment standard in AGENTS.md, which runtime_docs_tests.rs enforces.
